     1	/**
     2	 * AtomicStoreDO — Durable Object providing linearizable CAS for mppx 0.7.0.
     3	 *
     4	 * ## Why a Durable Object?
     5	 *
     6	 * Cloudflare KV has no server-side conditional write. A Worker-side
     7	 * read→transform→write on KV is non-atomic: two isolates can read the same
     8	 * value before either writes, so BOTH accept the same payment (double-spend
     9	 * on the charge replay-protection path) or the channel cumulative goes
    10	 * backwards (channel path).
    11	 *
    12	 * A Durable Object (DO) is a single-threaded JS isolate. The CF platform
    13	 * guarantees that for a given DO id, **only one `fetch` handler executes at
    14	 * a time** — all other concurrent requests are queued behind it. This turns
    15	 * the DO into a linearizable mutex over its storage without any application-
    16	 * level locking.
    17	 *
    18	 * Reference: https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/reference/
    19	 *            "At most one event handler runs at any given time."
    20	 *
    21	 * ## Key layout inside the DO's storage
    22	 *
    23	 * For each mppx store key `k`:
    24	 *   `v:<k>` → the stored value string (absent ≡ null)
    25	 *   `n:<k>` → the version counter as a number (absent ≡ 0)
    26	 *
    27	 * Separating value and version into two storage keys keeps the hot-path
    28	 * writes minimal (only `n:` bumped on every commit) and avoids marshalling
    29	 * the entire `{value, version}` object into a single JSON blob.
    30	 *
    31	 * ## Internal HTTP API (Worker → DO)
    32	 *
    33	 * All calls are POST to the DO's `fetch()` endpoint. The DO is never
    34	 * exposed to the public internet — it is only reachable via the
    35	 * `DurableObjectNamespace.get(id).fetch(...)` internal Cloudflare path.
    36	 *
    37	 * POST /read  — body: { key: string }
    38	 *               response: { value: string | null; version: number }
    39	 *
    40	 * POST /commit — body: { key: string; expectedVersion: number;
    41	 *                        op: 'set' | 'delete'; value?: string }
    42	 *               response: { ok: true }
    43	 *                       | { ok: false; value: string | null; version: number }
    44	 *
    45	 * If `storedVersion === expectedVersion` the write is applied and `version`
    46	 * is incremented to `expectedVersion + 1`. Otherwise the current (value,
    47	 * version) is returned so the caller can retry without an extra /read.
    48	 *
    49	 * ## Atomicity of the commit operation
    50	 *
    51	 * The entire get-compare-set inside `fetch()` is synchronous (no awaits
    52	 * between the storage.get calls and the storage.put/delete call except for
    53	 * the awaits inherent to the storage API itself). Because the DO is single-
    54	 * threaded and only one request runs at a time, no interleaving is possible.
    55	 *
    56	 * We use `storage.transaction()` on the /commit path to ensure the
    57	 * get and the conditional put/delete are treated as an atomic unit by
    58	 * the DO's durable storage layer as well (important for crash safety — the
    59	 * version bump and value write either both persist or neither does).
    60	 */
    61	
    62	export class AtomicStoreDO implements DurableObject {
    63	  private readonly storage: DurableObjectStorage
    64	
    65	  constructor(state: DurableObjectState) {
    66	    this.storage = state.storage
    67	  }
    68	
    69	  async fetch(request: Request): Promise<Response> {
    70	    const url = new URL(request.url)
    71	
    72	    if (request.method !== 'POST') {
    73	      return new Response('Method Not Allowed', { status: 405 })
    74	    }
    75	
    76	    let body: unknown
    77	    try {
    78	      body = await request.json()
    79	    } catch {
    80	      return new Response('Bad Request: invalid JSON', { status: 400 })
    81	    }
    82	
    83	    if (url.pathname === '/read') {
    84	      return this.handleRead(body)
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    if (url.pathname === '/commit') {
    88	      return this.handleCommit(body)
    89	    }
    90	
    91	    if (url.pathname === '/seed') {
    92	      return this.handleSeed(body)
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    return new Response('Not Found', { status: 404 })
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    99	  // /read handler
   100	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   101	
   102	  private async handleRead(body: unknown): Promise<Response> {
   103	    if (!isReadBody(body)) {
   104	      return new Response('Bad Request: expected { key: string }', { status: 400 })
   105	    }
   106	    const { key } = body
   107	
   108	    // Both reads are independent — no transaction needed for reads since we
   109	    // are single-threaded and there are no awaits between them that could
   110	    // interleave with a concurrent request.
   111	    const [value, version] = await Promise.all([
   112	      this.storage.get<string>(`v:${key}`),
   113	      this.storage.get<number>(`n:${key}`),
   114	    ])
   115	
   116	    const resp: ReadResponse = {
   117	      value: value ?? null,
   118	      version: version ?? 0,
   119	    }
   120	    return Response.json(resp)
   121	  }
   122	
   123	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   124	  // /commit handler
   125	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   126	
   127	  private async handleCommit(body: unknown): Promise<Response> {
   128	    if (!isCommitBody(body)) {
   129	      return new Response('Bad Request: expected CommitBody', { status: 400 })
   130	    }
   131	    const { key, expectedVersion, op, value: newValue } = body
   132	
   133	    // Use storage.transaction() so that the get + conditional put/delete are
   134	    // persisted as an atomic unit. If the DO crashes after the put but before
   135	    // the version bump, the transaction rolls back and the next request re-
   136	    // reads a consistent (old-value, old-version) — no split-brain state.
   137	    let committed = false
   138	    let currentValue: string | null = null
   139	    let currentVersion = 0
   140	
   141	    await this.storage.transaction(async (txn) => {
   142	      const [storedValue, storedVersion] = await Promise.all([
   143	        txn.get<string>(`v:${key}`),
   144	        txn.get<number>(`n:${key}`),
   145	      ])
   146	      currentValue = storedValue ?? null
   147	      currentVersion = storedVersion ?? 0
   148	
   149	      if (currentVersion !== expectedVersion) {
   150	        // Conflict — the caller's expectedVersion is stale. Roll back the
   151	        // transaction (no write). We return the fresh value+version below so
   152	        // the caller can re-run `fn` without a separate /read round trip.
   153	        txn.rollback()
   154	        return
   155	      }
   156	
   157	      // Version matches — apply the write.
   158	      if (op === 'set') {
   159	        if (newValue === undefined) {
   160	          txn.rollback()
   161	          throw new Error('AtomicStoreDO: op=set requires value')
   162	        }
   163	        await txn.put(`v:${key}`, newValue)
   164	      } else {
   165	        // op === 'delete'
   166	        await txn.delete(`v:${key}`)
   167	      }
   168	      await txn.put(`n:${key}`, expectedVersion + 1)
   169	      committed = true
   170	    })
   171	
   172	    if (committed) {
   173	      return Response.json({ ok: true } satisfies CommitOkResponse)
   174	    }
   175	
   176	    const resp: CommitConflictResponse = {
   177	      ok: false,
   178	      value: currentValue,
   179	      version: currentVersion,
   180	    }
   181	    return Response.json(resp)
   182	  }
   183	
   184	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   185	  // /seed handler — one-time migration helper
   186	  // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   187	  //
   188	  // Semantics: idempotent + non-destructive.
   189	  //   • Reads v:<key> inside a transaction.
   190	  //   • If the key ALREADY EXISTS → returns { seeded: false, reason: 'exists' }
   191	  //     WITHOUT writing anything. The live payment path may have already
   192	  //     written this key; we must never clobber it.
   193	  //   • If the key is ABSENT → writes v:<key>=value and n:<key>=1 atomically,
   194	  //     returns { seeded: true }.
   195	  //
   196	  // Using storage.transaction() ensures the read+conditional-write is crash-
   197	  // safe: if the DO dies after the put but before the version write the txn
   198	  // rolls back and the next /seed call re-checks from a clean state.
   199	  //
   200	  // Called exclusively from the Worker-side admin route — never from the
   201	  // public internet (the DO is not directly reachable externally).
   202	
   203	  private async handleSeed(body: unknown): Promise<Response> {
   204	    if (!isSeedBody(body)) {
   205	      return new Response('Bad Request: expected { key: string, value: string }', { status: 400 })
   206	    }
   207	    const { key, value } = body
   208	
   209	    let seeded = false
   210	
   211	    await this.storage.transaction(async (txn) => {
   212	      const existing = await txn.get<string>(`v:${key}`)
   213	      if (existing !== undefined && existing !== null) {
   214	        // Key already present — do nothing, leave live state untouched.
   215	        return
   216	      }
   217	      // Key is absent — safe to write the migrated value.
   218	      // We start version at 1 (matching what a first /commit with
   219	      // expectedVersion=0 would produce) so subsequent update() calls
   220	      // work without any special-casing.
   221	      await txn.put(`v:${key}`, value)
   222	      await txn.put(`n:${key}`, 1)
   223	      seeded = true
   224	    })
   225	
   226	    const resp: SeedResponse = seeded
   227	      ? { seeded: true }
   228	      : { seeded: false, reason: 'exists' }
   229	    return Response.json(resp)
   230	  }
   231	}
   232	
   233	// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   234	// Wire-format types and guards
   235	// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   236	
   237	export type ReadResponse = {
   238	  value: string | null
   239	  version: number
   240	}
   241	
   242	export type CommitOkResponse = {
   243	  ok: true
   244	}
   245	
   246	export type CommitConflictResponse = {
   247	  ok: false
   248	  value: string | null
   249	  version: number
   250	}
   251	
   252	export type CommitResponse = CommitOkResponse | CommitConflictResponse
   253	
   254	type ReadBody = { key: string }
   255	type CommitBody = {
   256	  key: string
   257	  expectedVersion: number
   258	  op: 'set' | 'delete'
   259	  value?: string
   260	}
     1	/**
     2	 * Durable Object–backed AtomicStore adapter for mppx 0.7.0.
     3	 *
     4	 * ## Problem with the previous KV implementation
     5	 *
     6	 * mppx 0.7.0's `@stellar/mpp` charge and channel constructors require
     7	 * `Store.AtomicStore` — a store whose `update(key, fn)` is a TRUE
     8	 * linearizable compare-and-set (CAS). Native Cloudflare KV has no
     9	 * server-side conditional write, so the old implementation was a
    10	 * non-atomic read→transform→write: two Worker isolates serving
    11	 * parallel requests could both read the same value before either
    12	 * writes, letting BOTH accept the same payment (double-spend on the
    13	 * charge replay-protection path) or allowing the channel cumulative
    14	 * to go backwards (channel path).
    15	 *
    16	 * ## Solution: Durable Object as a linearizable mutex
    17	 *
    18	 * A Cloudflare Durable Object (DO) is a single-threaded JS isolate.
    19	 * The CF platform guarantees: "At most one event handler runs at any
    20	 * given time." All requests to a given DO id are serialized by the
    21	 * platform — no application-level locking required.
    22	 *
    23	 * See src/mpp/atomic-store-do.ts for the DO class and its internal
    24	 * /read + /commit HTTP API.
    25	 *
    26	 * ## Optimistic CAS loop (the key insight)
    27	 *
    28	 * mppx's `fn` callback closes over per-request data and is not
    29	 * serialisable (cannot be sent to the DO). We run `fn` on the Worker
    30	 * side but make the read and conditional-write atomic on the DO side:
    31	 *
    32	 *   1. Worker asks DO: GET { value, version } for key.
    33	 *   2. Worker runs fn(value) locally → Change { op, value?, result }.
    34	 *   3. If op === 'noop' → return result (no write needed, no DO round trip).
    35	 *   4. If op === 'set' | 'delete' → Worker asks DO: COMMIT
    36	 *      { key, expectedVersion=version, op, value }.
    37	 *   5. DO (single-threaded) checks storedVersion === expectedVersion:
    38	 *        • Match → applies write, bumps version, returns { ok: true }.
    39	 *        • Mismatch → returns { ok: false, value: fresh, version: fresh }.
    40	 *   6. On conflict → back to step 2 with the fresh (value, version).
    41	 *      Loop is bounded by MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES.
    42	 *
    43	 * mppx's Store.d.ts explicitly states that `fn` "may be retried" and
    44	 * "must be side-effect-free" — so re-running it is contract-safe.
    45	 *
    46	 * ## Race-safety proof (two-parallel-request scenario)
    47	 *
    48	 * Suppose R1 and R2 arrive simultaneously for the same charge key
    49	 * (replay-protection scenario). Both find value=null, version=0.
    50	 *
    51	 *   • R1 /read  → { value: null, version: 0 }
    52	 *   • R2 /read  → { value: null, version: 0 }
    53	 *   • R1 fn(null) → { op:'set', value: pendingState, result: 'claimed' }
    54	 *   • R2 fn(null) → { op:'set', value: pendingState, result: 'claimed' }
    55	 *   • R1 /commit expectedVersion=0 → DO serialises: storedVersion=0 matches
    56	 *     → writes value, sets version=1 → { ok: true } → R1 returns 'claimed'.
    57	 *   • R2 /commit expectedVersion=0 → DO serialises: storedVersion is NOW 1
    58	 *     (R1 already bumped it) → mismatch → { ok: false, value: pendingState,
    59	 *     version: 1 }.
    60	 *   • R2 retries: fn(pendingState) → mppx returns { op:'noop', result:'replay' }
    61	 *     → R2 returns 'replay' without writing.
    62	 *
    63	 * One request claims, one gets replay. No double-spend.
    64	 *
    65	 * ## Why ALL operations go through the DO (not a KV+DO split)
    66	 *
    67	 * mppx's `get()` is called to read state that `update()` subsequently
    68	 * modifies. If `get()` reads from KV and `update()` reads from the DO,
    69	 * the two can diverge (KV eventual consistency). Routing everything
    70	 * through the DO ensures a single source of truth with no split-brain.
    71	 *
    72	 * ## Key-space notes
    73	 *
    74	 * The DO's internal storage uses `v:<k>` and `n:<k>` namespacing to
    75	 * separate value from version. The external mppx key `k` passes through
    76	 * unchanged (no extra prefix needed — the DO is dedicated to mppx storage
    77	 * and does not share its internal storage with any other subsystem).
    78	 *
    79	 * mppx's own key prefixes (`stellar:charge:*`, `stellar:channel:*`) do
    80	 * NOT collide with our sidecar keys (`stellarChannel:*`, `tempoChannel:*`,
    81	 * `idempotency:*`) which still live in KV — this file only replaces the
    82	 * mppx Store adapter, not the rest of KV usage.
    83	 *
    84	 * ## DO instance ID
    85	 *
    86	 * We use a single named DO `"mppx"` as the singleton for all mppx keys.
    87	 * This is intentional: mppx's charge and channel servers share the same
    88	 * Store and their keys must be mutually serialised (a replay-protection
    89	 * key for charge and a cumulative key for channel must not race each
    90	 * other either). One DO = one serialization point = correct.
    91	 *
    92	 * At mppx's current key cardinality (challenge IDs + per-channel
    93	 * cumulatives, measured in tens to low-hundreds of unique keys) a single
    94	 * DO instance has ample throughput. If traffic ever grows to thousands of
    95	 * concurrent payments, the DO can be sharded by key prefix — but that is
    96	 * not a concern for the current deployment size.
    97	 *
    98	 * See internaldocs/v2-stellar-channel-notes.md §N4 for the key-collision
    99	 * audit that confirms mppx's `stellar:charge:*` and `stellar:channel:*`
   100	 * key prefixes do not collide with any of our own KV keys.
   101	 */
   102	
   103	import type { Store } from 'mppx/server'
   104	import type { ReadResponse, CommitResponse } from './atomic-store-do'
   105	
   106	/**
   107	 * Maximum number of times `update()` will retry after a CAS conflict.
   108	 * A conflict means another DO request won the write between our /read
   109	 * and our /commit. mppx guarantees `fn` is side-effect-free and
   110	 * re-runnable, so each retry is safe. After MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES
   111	 * consecutive conflicts we throw — it is better to fail a payment
   112	 * than to loop infinitely or silently accept under contention.
   113	 *
   114	 * In practice, mppx's replay-protection `fn` returns 'noop' on the
   115	 * second invocation (when it sees the already-claimed state), so
   116	 * retries almost always resolve in one loop iteration. The bound is
   117	 * a safety net, not an expected code path.
   118	 */
   119	const MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES = 5
   120	
   121	/**
   122	 * Internal URL base used for DO-to-Worker RPC. The actual hostname is
   123	 * irrelevant — CF routes DO `stub.fetch()` calls internally and never
   124	 * makes an outbound network request. We use a stable fake origin so
   125	 * `new URL(path, DO_ORIGIN)` produces a valid URL object.
   126	 */
   127	const DO_ORIGIN = 'https://atomic-store.internal'
   128	
   129	/**
   130	 * Shape of the `cloudflare.AtomicParameters` type from mppx's `Store` namespace.
   131	 * Passing a value of this type to `Store.cloudflare()` returns `Store.AtomicStore`.
   132	 */
   133	type KvAtomicParams = {
   134	  get: (key: string) => Promise<unknown>
   135	  put: (key: string, value: string) => Promise<void>
   136	  delete: (key: string) => Promise<void>
   137	  update: <R>(
   138	    key: string,
   139	    fn: (current: string | null) => Store.Change<string, R>,
   140	  ) => Promise<R>
   141	}
   142	
   143	/**
   144	 * Build the `cloudflare.AtomicParameters` object backed by an `AtomicStoreDO`
   145	 * Durable Object namespace.
   146	 *
   147	 * All four operations (get, put, delete, update) are routed through the DO so
   148	 * there is a single source of truth — if `get()` read from KV and `update()`
   149	 * read from the DO they could diverge due to KV's eventual consistency.
   150	 *
   151	 * Pass the returned object to `Store.cloudflare(...)` to obtain an
   152	 * `AtomicStore` compatible with `@stellar/mpp@0.7.0`'s charge and channel
   153	 * server constructors.
   154	 *
   155	 * @param ns - The `ATOMIC_STORE` `DurableObjectNamespace` from `env`.
   156	 */
   157	export function doAtomicParams(ns: DurableObjectNamespace): KvAtomicParams {
   158	  // All mppx keys share a single DO instance, named "mppx".
   159	  // `idFromName` is deterministic and pure — the same name always resolves to
   160	  // the same DO instance across all Worker isolates in all colos.
   161	  const stub = ns.get(ns.idFromName('mppx'))
   162	
   163	  /** Internal helper: POST to the DO and parse the JSON response. */
   164	  async function doPost<T>(path: string, payload: unknown): Promise<T> {
   165	    const resp = await stub.fetch(new Request(`${DO_ORIGIN}${path}`, {
   166	      method: 'POST',
   167	      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
   168	      body: JSON.stringify(payload),
   169	    }))
   170	    if (!resp.ok) {
   171	      const text = await resp.text()
   172	      throw new Error(`AtomicStoreDO ${path} failed (${resp.status}): ${text}`)
   173	    }
   174	    return resp.json() as Promise<T>
   175	  }
   176	
   177	  return {
   178	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   179	    // get: read the current value (not version) for a key.
   180	    // Routes through the DO for consistency — see module-level comment.
   181	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   182	    async get(key: string): Promise<string | null> {
   183	      const r = await doPost<ReadResponse>('/read', { key })
   184	      return r.value
   185	    },
   186	
   187	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   188	    // put: overwrite a key unconditionally.
   189	    // Used by mppx for non-CAS writes (rare; most writes go through update).
   190	    // Implemented as a commit with expectedVersion=-1 which always conflicts
   191	    // with any real version (≥0). We bypass the version check by using a
   192	    // dedicated 'set' commit with a sentinel expectedVersion of -1.
   193	    //
   194	    // Alternative: add a /put endpoint to the DO. We chose to reuse /commit
   195	    // with expectedVersion=-1 to keep the DO API surface minimal — but the
   196	    // DO must accept -1. Looking at the DO impl: the check is
   197	    // `currentVersion !== expectedVersion`. A real version starts at 0.
   198	    // With expectedVersion=-1, currentVersion (0 if absent) !== -1, so it
   199	    // would CONFLICT. That means a blind put would loop and fail.
   200	    //
   201	    // The correct solution: add a /put endpoint to the DO for unconditional
   202	    // writes. We handle this by making put() its own /commit with a special
   203	    // "force" op. To keep the DO simple, we implement blind put here by
   204	    // re-reading the version first and then committing with that version.
   205	    // This is safe for the blind-put use case because mppx only uses put()
   206	    // in non-racing contexts (operator bootstrap, not the hot verify path).
   207	    //
   208	    // IMPORTANT: This is NOT atomic vs a concurrent update(). If you need
   209	    // strict ordering between put() and update(), use update() only.
   210	    // mppx's contract: put() is only called from places that don't race.
   211	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   212	    async put(key: string, value: string): Promise<void> {
   213	      // Read current version, then commit with that exact version.
   214	      // Retry loop mirrors update() in case of concurrent interference.
   215	      for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES; attempt++) {
   216	        const r = await doPost<ReadResponse>('/read', { key })
   217	        const result = await doPost<CommitResponse>('/commit', {
   218	          key,
   219	          expectedVersion: r.version,
   220	          op: 'set',
   221	          value,
   222	        })
   223	        if (result.ok) return
   224	        // Conflict: another writer modified the key between our read and
   225	        // commit. Retry with fresh version from the conflict response.
   226	        // (The conflict response already carries the fresh version.)
   227	      }
   228	      throw new Error(
   229	        `AtomicStoreDO put(${key}): exhausted ${MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES} retries — ` +
   230	        `too much concurrent write contention`,
   231	      )
   232	    },
   233	
   234	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   235	    // delete: remove a key unconditionally.
   236	    // Same semantics as put() — read-then-commit with retry.
   237	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   238	    async delete(key: string): Promise<void> {
   239	      for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES; attempt++) {
   240	        const r = await doPost<ReadResponse>('/read', { key })
   241	        if (r.value === null) return // already absent — nothing to do
   242	        const result = await doPost<CommitResponse>('/commit', {
   243	          key,
   244	          expectedVersion: r.version,
   245	          op: 'delete',
   246	        })
   247	        if (result.ok) return
   248	      }
   249	      throw new Error(
   250	        `AtomicStoreDO delete(${key}): exhausted ${MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES} retries — ` +
   251	        `too much concurrent write contention`,
   252	      )
   253	    },
   254	
   255	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   256	    // update: the core CAS operation.
   257	    //
   258	    // Optimistic loop (see module-level doc for full race proof):
   259	    //   1. /read  → { value, version }
   260	    //   2. fn(value) → change
   261	    //   3. If noop → return change.result (no write)
   262	    //   4. /commit with expectedVersion → ok OR conflict+fresh
   263	    //   5. On conflict → goto 2 with fresh (value, version)
   264	    // ------------------------------------------------------------------
   265	    async update<R>(
   266	      key: string,
   267	      fn: (current: string | null) => Store.Change<string, R>,
   268	    ): Promise<R> {
   269	      // Seed with a /read on the first iteration.
   270	      let { value, version } = await doPost<ReadResponse>('/read', { key })
   271	
   272	      for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES; attempt++) {
   273	        const change = fn(value)
   274	
   275	        if (change.op === 'noop') {
   276	          // No write needed — return immediately. This is the fast path for
   277	          // mppx's replay-protection check when it sees an already-claimed
   278	          // challenge: fn returns { op:'noop', result:'replay' }.
   279	          return change.result
   280	        }
   281	
   282	        // Attempt the conditional write.
   283	        const result = await doPost<CommitResponse>('/commit', {
   284	          key,
   285	          expectedVersion: version,
   286	          op: change.op,
   287	          ...(change.op === 'set' ? { value: change.value } : {}),
   288	        })
   289	
   290	        if (result.ok) {
   291	          // Committed successfully.
   292	          return change.result
   293	        }
   294	
   295	        // Conflict: between our /read and /commit, another request won
   296	        // the write. The DO returns the fresh (value, version) so we can
   297	        // re-run fn without a separate /read round trip.
   298	        value = result.value
   299	        version = result.version
   300	        // Loop: re-run fn with the updated value.
   301	      }
   302	
   303	      // We exhausted all retries. This is safer than silently accepting
   304	      // because on the charge path a stale noop-result could mean we
   305	      // mis-classify a replay as a fresh claim.
   306	      throw new Error(
   307	        `AtomicStoreDO update(${key}): exhausted ${MAX_UPDATE_RETRIES} CAS retries — ` +
   308	        `too much concurrent write contention on this key`,
   309	      )
   310	    },
   311	  }
   312	}
   313

   315	 * @deprecated Use `doAtomicParams(env.ATOMIC_STORE)` instead.
   316	 *
   317	 * The KV-backed implementation was non-atomic under multi-isolate
   318	 * Worker concurrency (two isolates could both read the same value
   319	 * before either writes → double-spend on the charge replay-protection
   320	 * path). Replaced by the Durable Object–backed `doAtomicParams` in

   263	  return (
   264	    typeof x === 'object' &&
   265	    x !== null &&
   266	    'key' in x &&
   267	    typeof (x as Record<string, unknown>).key === 'string'
   268	  )
   269	}
   270	
   271	function isCommitBody(x: unknown): x is CommitBody {
   272	  if (typeof x !== 'object' || x === null) return false
   273	  const o = x as Record<string, unknown>
   274	  if (typeof o.key !== 'string') return false
   275	  if (typeof o.expectedVersion !== 'number') return false
   276	  if (o.op !== 'set' && o.op !== 'delete') return false
   277	  if (o.op === 'set' && typeof o.value !== 'string') return false
   278	  return true
   279	}

export type StoreItemMap = Record<string, unknown>;
/**
 * Describes the outcome of an atomic {@link Update} callback.
 *
 * - `noop` — leave the stored value unchanged.
 * - `set`  — write `value` for the key.
 * - `delete` — remove the key.
 *
 * Every variant carries a `result` that is forwarded to the caller.
 */
export type Change<value, result> = {
    op: 'noop';
    result: result;
} | {
    op: 'set';
    value: value;
    result: result;
} | {
    op: 'delete';
    result: result;
};
/**
 * Atomic read-modify-write for a single key.
 *
 * `fn` receives the current value (or `null`) and returns a {@link Change}
 * describing the write to perform. Implementations may retry `fn`, so it
 * must be synchronous and free of side effects.
 */
export type Update<itemMap extends StoreItemMap = StoreItemMap> = <key extends keyof itemMap & string, result>(key: key, fn: (current: itemMap[key] | null) => Change<itemMap[key], result>) => Promise<result>;
/** Base key-value actions available on every {@link Store}. */
export type StoreActions<itemMap extends StoreItemMap = StoreItemMap> = {
    get: <key extends keyof itemMap & string>(key: key) => Promise<itemMap[key] | null>;
    put: <key extends keyof itemMap & string>(key: key, value: itemMap[key]) => Promise<void>;
    delete: <key extends keyof itemMap & string>(key: key) => Promise<void>;
};
/** Atomic actions that can be provided via the `extended` slot. */
export type AtomicActions<itemMap extends StoreItemMap = StoreItemMap> = {
    update: Update<itemMap>;
};
/**
 * Async key-value store.
 *
 * The second generic `extended` accumulates additional capabilities
 * (like {@link AtomicActions}) without structural patching.
 */
export type Store<itemMap extends StoreItemMap = StoreItemMap, extended extends Record<string, unknown> | undefined = undefined> = StoreActions<itemMap> & (extended extends Record<string, unknown> ? extended : unknown);
/**
 * A {@link Store} whose atomic {@link Update} method is guaranteed to exist.
 *
 * Use this when atomicity is required (e.g., replay protection, channel
 * deductions). Factory functions return `AtomicStore` when the backing
 * adapter provides an `update` implementation.
 *
 * Equivalent to `Store<itemMap, AtomicActions<itemMap>>`.
 */
export type AtomicStore<itemMap extends StoreItemMap = StoreItemMap> = Store<itemMap, AtomicActions<itemMap>>;
/** Options shared by store constructors. */
export type Options = {
    /** Prefix prepended to every backing store key. */
    keyPrefix?: string | undefined;
};
/** Creates a {@link Store} from an existing implementation. */
export declare function from<store extends AtomicStore<any>>(store: store, options?: Options): store;
export declare function from<store extends Store<any>>(store: store, options?: Options): store;
/** Wraps a Cloudflare KV namespace. */
export declare function cloudflare(kv: cloudflare.AtomicParameters, options?: cloudflare.Options): AtomicStore;
export declare function cloudflare(kv: cloudflare.Parameters, options?: cloudflare.Options): Store;
export declare namespace cloudflare {
    type Options = {
        /** Prefix prepended to every backing store key. */
        keyPrefix?: string | undefined;
    };
    type Parameters = {
        get: (key: string) => Promise<unknown>;
        put: (key: string, value: string) => Promise<void>;
        delete: (key: string) => Promise<void>;
        update?: <result>(key: string, fn: (current: string | null) => Change<string, result>) => Promise<result>;
    };
    type AtomicParameters = Omit<Parameters, 'update'> & {
        update: NonNullable<Parameters['update']>;
    };
}

        keyPrefix?: string | undefined;
    };
}

    };

        update: NonNullable<Parameters['update']>;
    };
}

//# sourceMappingURL=Store.d.ts.map/**
 * Async key-value store interface.
 *
 * Modeled after Cloudflare KV's API (`get`/`put`/`delete`).
 * Implementations handle serialization internally.
 *
 * ## Type architecture
 *
 * Uses a two-slot generic pattern inspired by Viem's `Client` type:
 *
 * - `itemMap` — constrains keys and their value types
 * - `extended` — accumulates additional capabilities (e.g., atomic `update`)
 *
 * `AtomicStore` is a type alias that fills the `extended` slot with
 * `AtomicActions`, just like Viem's `PublicClient = Client<..., PublicActions>`.
 */
import { Json } from 'ox';
const keyPrefixCache = new WeakMap();
export function from(store, options) {
    return withKeyPrefix(store, options?.keyPrefix);
}
function withKeyPrefix(store, keyPrefix = '') {
    if (!keyPrefix)
        return store;
    const cached = keyPrefixCache.get(store)?.get(keyPrefix);
    if (cached)
        return cached;
    const backing = store;
    const prefixedKey = (key) => `${keyPrefix}${key}`;
    const prefixed = from({
        async get(key) {
            return backing.get(prefixedKey(key));
        },
        async put(key, value) {
            await backing.put(prefixedKey(key), value);
        },
        async delete(key) {
            await backing.delete(prefixedKey(key));
        },
        ...('update' in store
            ? {
                async update(key, fn) {
                    return store.update(prefixedKey(key), fn);
                },
            }
            : {}),
    });
    const cachedByPrefix = keyPrefixCache.get(store) ?? new Map();
    cachedByPrefix.set(keyPrefix, prefixed);
    keyPrefixCache.set(store, cachedByPrefix);
    return prefixed;
}
function wrapJsonUpdate(update) {
    if (!update)
        return {};
    return {
        async update(key, fn) {
            return update(key, (current) => {
                const parsed = current == null ? null : Json.parse(current);
                const change = fn(parsed);
                if (change.op !== 'set')
                    return change;
                return { ...change, value: Json.stringify(change.value) };
            });
        },
    };
}
export function cloudflare(kv, options) {
    return from({
        async get(key) {
            const raw = await kv.get(key);
            if (raw == null)
                return null;
            return Json.parse(raw);
        },
        async put(key, value) {
            await kv.put(key, Json.stringify(value));
        },
        async delete(key) {
            await kv.delete(key);
        },
        ...wrapJsonUpdate(kv.update),
    }, options);
}
/** In-memory store backed by a `Map`. JSON-roundtrips values to match production behavior. */
export function memory(options) {
    const store = new Map();
    return from({
        async get(key) {
            const raw = store.get(key);
            if (raw === undefined)
                return null;
            return Json.parse(raw);
        },
        async put(key, value) {
            store.set(key, Json.stringify(value));
        },
        async delete(key) {
            store.delete(key);
        },
        async update(key, fn) {
            const current = store.has(key) ? Json.parse(store.get(key)) : null;
            const change = fn(current);
            if (change.op === 'set')
                store.set(key, Json.stringify(change.value));
            if (change.op === 'delete')
                store.delete(key);
            return change.result;
        },
    }, options);
}
export function redis(client, options) {
    return from({
        async get(key) {
            const raw = await client.get(key);
            if (raw == null)
                return null;
            return Json.parse(raw);
        },
        async put(key, value) {
            await client.set(key, Json.stringify(value));
        },
        async delete(key) {
            await client.del(key);
        },
        ...wrapJsonUpdate(client.update),
    }, options);
}

    return from({

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * In-process mock for `AtomicStoreDO` + `DurableObjectNamespace`.
     3	 *
     4	 * Used in Vitest tests to provide a real, working implementation of
     5	 * `ATOMIC_STORE` without needing the Cloudflare runtime. The mock
     6	 * implements the same /read + /commit HTTP API as the real DO but
     7	 * runs it directly in memory via `AtomicStoreDO` (the actual class).
     8	 *
     9	 * Why instantiate the real class rather than a stub?
    10	 * This ensures tests exercise the exact same version-CAS logic that
    11	 * runs in production. A stub that just returns ok=true would not catch
    12	 * type errors or behavioral bugs in the commit handler.
    13	 *
    14	 * The only thing we mock is the `DurableObjectState` constructor
    15	 * argument — we provide an in-memory `DurableObjectStorage` whose
    16	 * get/put/delete/transaction behave the same as the real CF storage
    17	 * (modulo actual durability, which is irrelevant for unit tests).
    18	 */
    19	
    20	import { AtomicStoreDO } from '../../src/mpp/atomic-store-do'
    21	
    22	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    23	// In-memory DurableObjectStorage implementation for tests
    24	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * Minimal in-memory `DurableObjectStorage` that supports the operations
    28	 * used by `AtomicStoreDO`: get, put, delete, and transaction.
    29	 *
    30	 * `transaction()` is synchronous-over-async via a promise chain — adequate
    31	 * for tests where we control concurrency. The real CF runtime serialises
    32	 * requests at the DO boundary; in tests we're single-threaded anyway.
    33	 */
    34	class InMemoryStorage {
    35	  private readonly store = new Map<string, unknown>()
    36	
    37	  async get<T>(key: string): Promise<T | undefined>
    38	  async get<T>(keys: string[]): Promise<Map<string, T>>
    39	  async get<T>(keyOrKeys: string | string[]): Promise<T | undefined | Map<string, T>> {
    40	    if (Array.isArray(keyOrKeys)) {
    41	      const result = new Map<string, T>()
    42	      for (const k of keyOrKeys) {
    43	        const v = this.store.get(k)
    44	        if (v !== undefined) result.set(k, v as T)
    45	      }
    46	      return result
    47	    }
    48	    return this.store.get(keyOrKeys) as T | undefined
    49	  }
    50	
    51	  async put<T>(key: string, value: T): Promise<void>
    52	  async put<T>(entries: Record<string, T>): Promise<void>
    53	  async put<T>(keyOrEntries: string | Record<string, T>, value?: T): Promise<void> {
    54	    if (typeof keyOrEntries === 'string') {
    55	      this.store.set(keyOrEntries, value)
    56	    } else {
    57	      for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(keyOrEntries)) {
    58	        this.store.set(k, v)
    59	      }
    60	    }
    61	  }
    62	
    63	  async delete(key: string): Promise<boolean>
    64	  async delete(keys: string[]): Promise<number>
    65	  async delete(keyOrKeys: string | string[]): Promise<boolean | number> {
    66	    if (Array.isArray(keyOrKeys)) {
    67	      let count = 0
    68	      for (const k of keyOrKeys) if (this.store.delete(k)) count++
    69	      return count
    70	    }
    71	    return this.store.delete(keyOrKeys)
    72	  }
    73	
    74	  /**
    75	   * In tests, `transaction()` runs the closure directly on the backing store
    76	   * with a `rollback()` that clears a dirty set. Full serialisation is
    77	   * guaranteed by the JS event loop in tests (no true concurrent requests).
    78	   */
    79	  async transaction<T>(
    80	    closure: (txn: DurableObjectTransaction) => Promise<T>,
    81	  ): Promise<T> {
    82	    // Track writes made during the transaction so rollback() can undo them.
    83	    const written = new Map<string, { prev: unknown; existed: boolean }>()
    84	    let rolledBack = false
    85	
    86	    const txn: DurableObjectTransaction = {
    87	      get: this.get.bind(this) as DurableObjectTransaction['get'],
    88	      put: async <V>(keyOrEntries: string | Record<string, V>, value?: V): Promise<void> => {
    89	        if (typeof keyOrEntries === 'string') {
    90	          if (!written.has(keyOrEntries)) {
    91	            written.set(keyOrEntries, {
    92	              prev: this.store.get(keyOrEntries),
    93	              existed: this.store.has(keyOrEntries),
    94	            })
    95	          }
    96	          this.store.set(keyOrEntries, value)
    97	        } else {
    98	          for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(keyOrEntries)) {
    99	            if (!written.has(k)) {
   100	              written.set(k, { prev: this.store.get(k), existed: this.store.has(k) })
   101	            }
   102	            this.store.set(k, v)
   103	          }
   104	        }
   105	      },
   106	      delete: async (keyOrKeys: string | string[]): Promise<boolean | number> => {
   107	        if (Array.isArray(keyOrKeys)) {
   108	          let count = 0
   109	          for (const k of keyOrKeys) {
   110	            if (!written.has(k)) {
   111	              written.set(k, { prev: this.store.get(k), existed: this.store.has(k) })
   112	            }
   113	            if (this.store.delete(k)) count++
   114	          }
   115	          return count
   116	        }
   117	        if (!written.has(keyOrKeys)) {
   118	          written.set(keyOrKeys, { prev: this.store.get(keyOrKeys), existed: this.store.has(keyOrKeys) })
   119	        }
   120	        return this.store.delete(keyOrKeys)
   121	      },
   122	      rollback: () => {
   123	        rolledBack = true
   124	        // Undo all writes made during this transaction.
   125	        for (const [k, { prev, existed }] of written) {
   126	          if (existed) {
   127	            this.store.set(k, prev)
   128	          } else {
   129	            this.store.delete(k)
   130	          }
   131	        }
   132	      },
   133	      list: async () => new Map(),
   134	      getAlarm: async () => null,
   135	      setAlarm: async () => {},
   136	      deleteAlarm: async () => {},
   137	    } as unknown as DurableObjectTransaction
   138	
   139	    const result = await closure(txn)
   140	    if (rolledBack) {
   141	      // Undo: already handled inside rollback()
   142	    }
   143	    return result
   144	  }
   145	
   146	  async deleteAll(): Promise<void> {
   147	    this.store.clear()
   148	  }
   149	
   150	  // Unused stubs required by the type
   151	  async list(): Promise<Map<string, unknown>> { return new Map() }
   152	  async getAlarm(): Promise<null> { return null }
   153	  async setAlarm(): Promise<void> {}
   154	  async deleteAlarm(): Promise<void> {}
   155	  async sync(): Promise<void> {}
   156	}
   157	
   158	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   159	// In-memory DurableObjectNamespace mock
   160	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   162	/**
   163	 * Build a `DurableObjectNamespace` mock that routes `stub.fetch()` calls
   164	 * to an in-process `AtomicStoreDO` instance backed by `InMemoryStorage`.
   165	 *
   166	 * One instance is shared across the lifetime of a test (same as one named
   167	 * DO `"mppx"` shared across requests in production).
   168	 *
   169	 * Usage in a test's makeEnv():
   170	 *
   171	 *   import { makeAtomicStoreMock } from './helpers/atomic-store-mock'
   172	 *   ...
   173	 *   ATOMIC_STORE: makeAtomicStoreMock(),
   174	 */
   175	export function makeAtomicStoreMock(): DurableObjectNamespace {
   176	  const storage = new InMemoryStorage()
   177	  const doState = {
   178	    storage,
   179	    id: { toString: () => 'mock-do-id', equals: () => true, name: 'mppx' },
   180	    blockConcurrencyWhile: async <T>(fn: () => Promise<T>) => fn(),
   181	    waitUntil: (_p: Promise<unknown>) => {},
   182	  } as unknown as DurableObjectState
   183	
   184	  // Instantiate the real DO class — this is what runs in production.
   185	  const doInstance = new AtomicStoreDO(doState)
   186	
   187	  // Stable mock ID.
   188	  const mockId: DurableObjectId = {
   189	    toString: () => 'mock-do-id',
   190	    equals: (other) => other.toString() === 'mock-do-id',
   191	    name: 'mppx',
   192	  }
   193	
   194	  // Stub that routes fetch() to the DO instance.
   195	  const stub: DurableObjectStub = {
   196	    id: mockId,
   197	    fetch: (input: RequestInfo | URL, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> => {
   198	      const req = input instanceof Request ? input : new Request(input, init)
   199	      return doInstance.fetch(req)
   200	    },
   201	  } as unknown as DurableObjectStub
   202	
   203	  const ns: DurableObjectNamespace = {
   204	    idFromName: (_name: string) => mockId,
   205	    idFromString: (_id: string) => mockId,
   206	    newUniqueId: () => mockId,
   207	    get: (_id: DurableObjectId) => stub,
   208	    jurisdiction: (_j: string) => ns,
   209	  } as unknown as DurableObjectNamespace
   210	
   211	  return ns
   212	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Tempo MPP Client — pays merchants on Tempo L2.
     3	 *
     4	 * Two modes:
     5	 *
     6	 * 1. Charge (`payMerchant`) — single-shot 402 settle per request.
     7	 *    Used by fixed-price merchants (Firecrawl, Exa, Parallel). mppx
     8	 *    client auto-handles the challenge: 402 → sign TIP-20 transfer
     9	 *    from Router's EVM wallet → retry with credential → 200.
    10	 *
    11	 * 2. Session (`payMerchantSession`) — long-lived payment channel
    12	 *    with streaming cumulative vouchers. Used by dynamic-price
    13	 *    merchants (OpenRouter). The channel is opened ONCE on-chain
    14	 *    via `scripts/open-channel.ts`, then every agent request
    15	 *    bumps an off-chain cumulative voucher signed by the Router.
    16	 *    mppx still handles the 402 dance; we supply the manual
    17	 *    voucher context via the `onChallenge` hook.
    18	 *
    19	 * Why both on one client: the `Mppx.create` API registers a list
    20	 * of methods and dispatches on the merchant's challenge intent
    21	 * (`charge` vs `session`), so a single client can handle either
    22	 * kind of merchant. We keep them in separate helpers because the
    23	 * session path needs to read + write channel state in KV on every
    24	 * request, and the charge path should not pay that overhead.
    25	 */
    26	
    27	import { Mppx, tempo, sessionLegacy as legacySession } from 'mppx/client'
    28	import { privateKeyToAccount } from 'viem/accounts'
    29	import type { Env } from '../index'
    30	import {
    31	  bumpCumulative,
    32	  getTempoChannel,
    33	  type TempoChannelState,
    34	} from './channel-store'
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * Minimal shape of the ChannelEntry that mppx passes to the
    38	 * `onChannelUpdate` callback. Defined here rather than imported
    39	 * from mppx internals because that path is a non-public internal
    40	 * module (see session-support-plan.md §8 risks). If a future mppx
    41	 * release moves or renames the type we only need to update this
    42	 * local stub, not a deep import path.
    43	 *
    44	 * In mppx 0.7.0 the TIP-1034 session path includes `descriptor` on
    45	 * the entry (shape: { authorizedSigner, expiringNonceHash, operator,
    46	 * payee, payer, salt, token }). The legacy session entry does not
    47	 * include it. We declare it optional so both paths share this type.
    48	 */
    49	type ChannelEntryLike = {
    50	  channelId: `0x${string}`
    51	  cumulativeAmount: bigint
    52	  escrowContract?: `0x${string}`
    53	  chainId: number
    54	  opened: boolean
    55	  descriptor?: {
    56	    authorizedSigner: `0x${string}`
    57	    expiringNonceHash: `0x${string}`
    58	    operator: `0x${string}`
    59	    payee: `0x${string}`
    60	    payer: `0x${string}`
    61	    salt: `0x${string}`
    62	    token: `0x${string}`
    63	  }
    64	}
    65	
    66	/**
    67	 * Error thrown by `payMerchantSession` when a merchant has not had
    68	 * its channel opened yet. The proxy catches this and returns a 503
    69	 * to the agent with a clear "session not installed" message so the
    70	 * operator knows to run `scripts/open-channel.ts`.
    71	 */
    72	export class ChannelNotInstalledError extends Error {
    73	  constructor(public readonly merchantId: string) {
    74	    super(
    75	      `No Tempo channel installed for merchant "${merchantId}". ` +
    76	        `Run scripts/open-channel.ts to open one.`,
    77	    )
    78	    this.name = 'ChannelNotInstalledError'
    79	  }
    80	}
    81	
    82	/**
    83	 * Build an mppx client that can handle both tempo.charge and
    84	 * tempo.session challenges.
    85	 *
    86	 * In manual session mode we pass an `onChannelUpdate` callback
    87	 * that mppx calls after every voucher sign; that's where we
    88	 * persist the new cumulative watermark back to KV. Without this
    89	 * callback a cold Worker restart would sign a voucher using the
    90	 * stored cumulative and immediately get rejected by the merchant
    91	 * ("non-monotonic cumulative") because the merchant already has
    92	 * a higher cumulative from the previous voucher.
    93	 *
    94	 * The charge method has no equivalent callback — it's stateless
    95	 * per request, which is exactly why it fits a Worker cleanly.
    96	 */
    97	function createTempoClientInternal(
    98	  env: Env,
    99	  opts: {
   100	    /**
   101	     * onChallenge hook forwarded to Mppx.create. Used by the
   102	     * session path to compute `cumulativeAmountRaw = stored + delta`
   103	     * at the moment the 402 arrives, and to call `createCredential`
   104	     * with the manual voucher context.
   105	     */
   106	    onChallenge?: Parameters<typeof Mppx.create>[0]['onChallenge']
   107	    /**
   108	     * Called by mppx after every successful voucher sign. We use
   109	     * it to bump the KV watermark. mppx passes `cumulativeAmount`
   110	     * as a bigint here, which matches what the KV store already
   111	     * stores as a base-unit string.
   112	     */
   113	    onChannelUpdate?: (entry: ChannelEntryLike) => void | Promise<void>
   114	  } = {},
   115	) {
   116	  const account = privateKeyToAccount(env.TEMPO_ROUTER_PRIVATE_KEY as `0x${string}`)
   117	
   118	  // Three methods registered:
   119	  //
   120	  // 1. `tempo.charge` — single-shot intent, unchanged.
   121	  //
   122	  // 2. `tempo.session(...)` (TIP-1034, `sessionMethod` in 0.7.0) — handles
   123	  //    merchants that advertise `sessionProtocol: 'v2'` in their 402
   124	  //    challenge. This is what anthropic, openai, gemini, openrouter now
   125	  //    send. In manual mode (no `deposit` param), mppx dispatches on
   126	  //    `canHandleChallenge` → v2 → this method, then calls `onChallenge`
   127	  //    where we supply `{action, channelId, cumulativeAmountRaw, descriptor}`.
   128	  //    The `descriptor` field is required by TIP-1034 — absent in 0.4.12,
   129	  //    the root cause of the bug.
   130	  //
   131	  // 3. `legacySession(...)` (legacy contract-backed session, was `session`
   132	  //    in 0.4.12, now re-exported as `sessionLegacy` in 0.7.0) — handles
   133	  //    merchants that advertise `sessionProtocol: 'v1'` or omit it. Provides
   134	  //    backward compatibility for any merchant still on the old protocol.
   135	  //    Its manual-mode context does NOT require `descriptor`.
   136	  //
   137	  // mppx dispatches on `canHandleChallenge` to pick the right method:
   138	  //   v2 challenge → tempo.session (TIP-1034)
   139	  //   v1 / absent  → legacySession
   140	  //
   141	  // No `deposit` parameter on either session method — that parameter is
   142	  // what flips them into auto mode. Leaving it unset keeps us in manual
   143	  // mode where our `onChallenge` hook controls every voucher.
   144	  const mppx = Mppx.create({
   145	    methods: [
   146	      tempo.charge({ account }),
   147	      tempo.session({
   148	        account,
   149	        onChannelUpdate: opts.onChannelUpdate,
   150	      }),
   151	      legacySession({
   152	        account,
   153	        onChannelUpdate: opts.onChannelUpdate as any,
   154	      }),
   155	    ],
   156	    polyfill: false,
   157	    onChallenge: opts.onChallenge,
   158	  } as any)
   159	
   160	  return mppx
   161	}
   162	
   163	/**
   164	 * Pay a Tempo merchant for a single fixed-price request. The
   165	 * mppx client handles the full 402 dance automatically via the
   166	 * registered `tempo.charge` method.
   167	 *
   168	 * Unchanged from the pre-session Router — callers that speak
   169	 * tempo.charge (Firecrawl, Exa, Parallel) must NOT switch to
   170	 * `payMerchantSession`.
   171	 */
   172	export async function payMerchant(
   173	  env: Env,
   174	  merchantUrl: string,
   175	  init?: RequestInit,
   176	): Promise<Response> {
   177	  const client = createTempoClientInternal(env)
   178	  return client.fetch(merchantUrl, init)
   179	}
   180	
   181	/**
   182	 * Add two base-unit cumulative amounts using BigInt so we don't
   183	 * lose precision on amounts above 2^53. Both inputs and output
   184	 * are non-negative integer strings.
   185	 */
   186	function addRaw(a: string, bRaw: string): string {
   187	  return (BigInt(a) + BigInt(bRaw)).toString()
   188	}
   189	
   190	/**
   191	 * Pay a Tempo merchant through an existing payment channel.
   192	 *
   193	 * Prerequisites:
   194	 *   - `scripts/open-channel.ts` has been run for this merchantId
   195	 *     and the resulting `TempoChannelState` is in KV.
   196	 *   - The merchant speaks `tempo.session` on this route.
   197	 *
   198	 * Flow:
   199	 *   1. Load the current channel state from KV — need the stored
   200	 *      cumulativeRaw to compute the new voucher amount.
   201	 *   2. Build a fresh mppx client for this request. The client's
   202	 *      `onChallenge` hook intercepts the merchant's 402 challenge,
   203	 *      reads the merchant-requested delta from it, computes
   204	 *      `newCumulativeRaw = stored + delta`, and creates a
   205	 *      manual-mode voucher credential.
   206	 *   3. mppx retries the request with that credential and returns
   207	 *      the merchant's final response.
   208	 *   4. `onChannelUpdate` fires after the voucher is signed and
   209	 *      bumps the KV watermark to the new cumulative.
   210	 *
   211	 * IMPORTANT: step 4 runs BEFORE we know whether the merchant
   212	 * returned 2xx or 5xx. See §5 of session-support-plan.md — the
   213	 * commit-after-2xx rule is enforced by the proxy layer, not
   214	 * here. This helper will happily bump the cumulative for a
   215	 * failed upstream request; the proxy either (a) only calls
   216	 * this helper after Stellar settle has succeeded and trusts
   217	 * that the next in-flight request will pick up the new watermark,
   218	 * or (b) rolls the watermark forward on success by passing a
   219	 * callback the proxy controls. We go with (a) because rollback
   220	 * in an eventually-consistent KV is harder to get right than
   221	 * "next voucher starts from wherever we landed, good or bad."
   222	 *
   223	 * The caller is responsible for handling ChannelNotInstalledError.
   224	 */
   225	export async function payMerchantSession(
   226	  env: Env,
   227	  merchantId: string,
   228	  merchantUrl: string,
   229	  init: RequestInit = {},
   230	): Promise<{ response: Response; channelBefore: TempoChannelState }> {
   231	  const channel = await getTempoChannel(env, merchantId)
   232	  if (!channel) {
   233	    throw new ChannelNotInstalledError(merchantId)
   234	  }
   235	
   236	  const client = createTempoClientInternal(env, {
   237	    // Intercept the merchant's 402 challenge so we can compute the
   238	    // cumulative-after-this-request ourselves. mppx then signs a
   239	    // voucher with that value and retries.
   240	    onChallenge: async (challenge, { createCredential }) => {
   241	      // Session challenge request shape (after the zod transform
   242	      // in tempo.session's request schema): amount is a base-unit
   243	      // string like "10000" for $0.01 at 6 decimals.
   244	      const delta = (challenge as any).request?.amount as string | undefined
   245	      if (!delta || !/^\d+$/.test(delta)) {
   246	        throw new Error(
   247	          `tempo.session challenge for ${merchantId} missing valid base-unit amount: ${delta}`,
   248	        )
   249	      }
   250	      const newCumulativeRaw = addRaw(channel.cumulativeRaw, delta)
   251	      // Manual-mode context: tell mppx "sign a voucher action on
   252	      // channel X at the new cumulative".
   253	      //
   254	      // For TIP-1034 (v2) merchants, `descriptor` is REQUIRED in the
   255	      // context — the TIP-1034 session method (`tempo.session`) needs
   256	      // it to derive the on-chain channel ID and sign the voucher.
   257	      // `descriptor` is captured at channel open time by
   258	      // `scripts/admin/open-tempo-channel.ts` via `onChannelUpdate`
   259	      // and stored in KV alongside `channelId` and `cumulativeRaw`.
   260	      //
   261	      // For legacy (v1) merchants, `descriptor` is absent from KV
   262	      // entries opened with pre-0.7.0 mppx. The `legacySession` method
   263	      // handles their challenges without requiring `descriptor`.
   264	      // Including it when present is harmless; legacySession ignores it.
   265	      return createCredential({
   266	        action: 'voucher',
   267	        channelId: channel.channelId,
   268	        cumulativeAmountRaw: newCumulativeRaw,
   269	        ...(channel.descriptor ? { descriptor: channel.descriptor } : {}),
   270	      } as any)
   271	    },
   272	    onChannelUpdate: async (entry: ChannelEntryLike) => {
   273	      // mppx gives us the just-signed cumulative as a bigint.
   274	      // Persist it to KV so a cold-isolate restart sees the
   275	      // new watermark.
   276	      //
   277	      // We use `bumpCumulative` (monotone) instead of a blind
   278	      // put so the KV side is safe even if two in-flight
   279	      // requests race: the slower write will see an equal or
   280	      // higher value and drop silently rather than rewinding.
   281	      await bumpCumulative(env, merchantId, entry.cumulativeAmount.toString())
   282	    },
   283	  })
   284	
   285	  const response = await client.fetch(merchantUrl, init)
   286	  return { response, channelBefore: channel }
   287	}
   288	
   289	/**
   290	 * Backwards-compat export — existing callers of `createTempoClient`
   291	 * in scripts/tests should keep working. The new signature keeps
   292	 * the single-arg form that just returns a vanilla client with
   293	 * both methods registered.
   294	 */
   295	export function createTempoClient(env: Env) {
   296	  return createTempoClientInternal(env)
   297	}

  1320	    stellarAmount = baseUnitsToDecimalString(parsed.request.amount, merchantDecimals)
  1321	  } catch (err: any) {
  1322	    return new Response(JSON.stringify({
  1323	      error: 'Could not normalize merchant amount',
  1324	      detail: err.message,
  1325	    }), {
  1326	      status: 502,
  1327	      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  1328	    })
  1329	  }
  1330	
  1331	  // Dispatch the actual verify on authKind. Both the charge and the
  1332	  // channel paths are HMAC-bound challenge verifications that return
  1333	  // a Method.Server output with `.status`, `.challenge`, and
  1334	  // `.withReceipt`, so downstream code can treat them uniformly.
  1335	  //
  1336	  // The shape of the arguments differs slightly:
  1337	  //   - charge:  { amount, currency, recipient, meta }
  1338	  //   - channel: { amount, channel, methodDetails }
  1339	  // The channel path doesn't take currency + recipient because those
  1340	  // are baked into the on-chain channel contract at deploy time.
  1341	  let verifyResult
  1342	  try {
  1343	    if (authKind === 'stellar.channel') {
  1344	      // Should not happen — if we got to this branch, resolve already
  1345	      // set channelContractForVerify. Defensive check for the type
  1346	      // narrowing below.
  1347	      if (!channelContractForVerify) {
  1348	        throw new Error('Internal: stellar.channel authKind without resolved contract')
  1349	      }
  1350	      // FX conversion: if the channel was opened against native XLM
  1351	      // (instead of USDC SAC), the merchant's USDC-denominated
  1352	      // amount has to be re-priced into XLM at a fixed rate before
  1353	      // the agent signs a voucher. Otherwise the router silently
  1354	      // bleeds value as a broker because 1 stroop XLM ≠ 1 base unit
  1355	      // USDC. USDC SAC channels (e.g. agent2's CAYS2LBU…) need no
  1356	      // conversion. See internaldocs/v2-todo.md#c and
  1357	      // v2-session-session-done.md §5.1 for the broker math.
  1358	      let channelAmount = stellarAmount
  1359	      if (channelCurrencyForVerify === STELLAR_NATIVE_XLM_SAC) {
  1360	        const rate = parseFloat(env.XLM_USD_RATE)
  1361	        if (!Number.isFinite(rate) || rate <= 0) {
  1362	          return new Response(JSON.stringify({
  1363	            error: 'XLM_USD_RATE misconfigured',
  1364	            detail: `Worker env XLM_USD_RATE must be a positive number, got ${env.XLM_USD_RATE}`,
  1365	          }), {
  1366	            status: 500,
  1367	            headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  1368	          })
  1369	        }
  1370	        try {
  1371	          channelAmount = convertUsdcToXlm(stellarAmount, rate)
  1372	        } catch (err: any) {
  1373	          return new Response(JSON.stringify({
  1374	            error: 'Could not convert USDC amount to XLM',
  1375	            detail: err.message,
  1376	          }), {
  1377	            status: 502,
  1378	            headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  1379	          })
  1380	        }
  1381	        console.log(
  1382	          `[proxy] XLM channel: converted ${stellarAmount} USDC -> ${channelAmount} XLM at rate ${rate}`,
  1383	        )
  1384	      }
  1385	      verifyResult = await (mppx as any)['stellar/channel']({
  1386	        amount: channelAmount,
  1387	        channel: channelContractForVerify,
  1388	        methodDetails: {
  1389	          reference: parsed.id,
  1390	        },
  1391	      })(mppxInput)
  1392	    } else {
  1393	      verifyResult = await (mppx as any)['stellar/charge']({
  1394	        amount: stellarAmount,
  1395	        currency: getStellarUsdcSac(env),
  1396	        recipient: getRouterStellarAddress(env),
  1397	        // `meta` is server-defined correlation data that mppx 0.7 binds into
  1398	        // the challenge `opaque` and re-checks at verify time (it MUST be
  1399	        // identical between issue and verify, or the credential is rejected
  1400	        // with "credential opaque does not match this route's requirements").
  1401	        // So meta may ONLY contain values that are stable across the two 402
  1402	        // round-trips for the same payment. `route.id` is stable; the upstream
  1403	        // merchant challenge id (`parsed.id`) is NOT — the merchant issues a
  1404	        // fresh challenge id on every probe, so putting it here made the
  1405	        // verify-time opaque diverge from the issue-time opaque and broke all
  1406	        // charge payments after the mppx 0.4->0.7 upgrade. Reconciliation with
  1407	        // the upstream challenge is done elsewhere (the proxy logs/links the
  1408	        // ids per request); it must not ride in the HMAC-bound opaque.
  1409	        meta: {
  1410	          route: route.id,
  1411	        },
  1412	      })(mppxInput)
  1413	    }
  1414	  } catch (err: any) {
  1415	    console.error(`[proxy] Stellar verify threw: ${err.message}`)
  1416	    return new Response(JSON.stringify({
  1417	      error: 'Payment verification failed',
  1418	      detail: err.message,
  1419	    }), {
  1420	      status: 402,
  1421	      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  1422	    })
  1423	  }
  1424	
  1425	  if (verifyResult.status === 402) {
  1426	    // Either no credential was presented, or it failed verification —
  1427	    // including replay attempts. The stellar charge method inside mppx
  1428	    // uses the shared KV store (see src/mpp/stellar-server.ts) to
  1429	    // reject any credential whose challenge id (and, for push mode, tx
  1430	    // hash) has already been settled. Replay protection is enforced
  1431	    // here, not via a counter, so we do not need an extra guard.
  1432	    //
  1433	    // NOTE: Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent, so two concurrent
  1434	    // replays *can* race past the dedup check within a narrow window.
  1435	    // For strict once-and-only-once semantics, migrate MPP_STORE to a
  1436	    // Durable Object. For now, the mppx store is the authoritative
  1437	    // replay guard and the window is small enough that the economic
  1438	    // loss is bounded to a single concurrent duplicate.
  1439	    //
  1440	    // Dual-format 402 injection: when X402_ENABLED, we add a
  1441	    // standard x402 `Payment-Required` header to the same response
  1442	    // so vanilla x402 clients (which call
  1443	    // `x402HTTPClient.getPaymentRequiredResponse`) can read the
  1444	    // challenge without parsing the mppx-flavored
  1445	    // `WWW-Authenticate` format. mppx clients ignore the new
  1446	    // header and keep using `WWW-Authenticate` exactly as before.
  1447	    // The merchant amount comes from `parsed.request.amount`
  1448	    // (Tempo USDC 6dp) and gets converted to Stellar 7dp inside
  1449	    // `buildX402PaymentRequiredHeader` (×10).
  1450	    //
  1451	    // This injection only fires on the FIRST probe (no credential
  1452	    // / failed credential). Subsequent retries with a valid
  1453	    // credential never reach this code.
  1454	    let merchantQuoteTempo: bigint | null = null
  1455	    try {

    /** Active channel ID, when available. */
    channelId: Hex.Hex | null;
    /** Local cumulative voucher authorization in raw token units. */
    cumulative: bigint;
};
/** Serializable client-side channel snapshot stored between manager instances. */
export type StoredSessionChannel = {
    /** Latest known channel ID. Used as the next request's channel hint. */
    channelId: Hex.Hex;
    /** Latest local cumulative voucher authorization in raw token units. */
    cumulativeAmount: string;
    /** Latest known deposit in raw token units. */
    deposit: string;
    /** TIP-1034 channel descriptor, used as a fallback when the server cannot snapshot. */
    descriptor: ChannelDescriptor;
    /** Escrow address used to derive the channel ID. */
    escrow: Address;
    /** Chain ID used to derive the channel ID. */
    chainId: number;
    /** Whether the channel was open when stored. Closed channels are not used as hints. */
    opened: boolean;
    /** Client timestamp for debugging or app-level eviction. */
    updatedAt: number;
};
/** Optional per-manager store for channel hints and restart recovery. */
export type SessionStore = {
    /** Reads the latest stored channel snapshot for this manager scope. */
    get(): Promise<StoredSessionChannel | null | undefined> | StoredSessionChannel | null | undefined;
    /** Persists the latest channel snapshot. */
    set(channel: StoredSessionChannel): Promise<void> | void;
    /** Deletes the stored snapshot when a channel is closed, when supported. */
        /** Address authorized to sign vouchers. Defaults to the account access key address when available, otherwise the account address. */
        authorizedSigner?: Address | undefined;
        /** Enables same-route HEAD bootstrap from a server session snapshot before opening a new channel. */
        bootstrap?: boolean | undefined;
        /** Viem client instance. Shorthand for `getClient: () => client`. */
        client?: import('viem').Client | undefined;
        /** Token decimals used to convert `maxDeposit` to raw units. Defaults to `6`. */
        decimals?: number | undefined;
        /** TIP20EscrowChannel precompile address override. */
        escrow?: Address | undefined;
        /** Fetch implementation used for HTTP probes, management posts, and paid retries. */
        fetch?: typeof globalThis.fetch | undefined;
        /** Maximum deposit in human-readable units (e.g. `'10'` for 10 tokens). Converted to raw units via `decimals`. */
        maxDeposit?: string | undefined;
        /** Optional per-manager store for persisted channel hints and restart recovery. */
        sessionStore?: SessionStore | undefined;
        /** Optional websocket constructor for runtimes without a global WebSocket. */
        webSocket?: WebSocketConstructor | undefined;
    };
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
name = "mpprouter"
main = "src/index.ts"
compatibility_date = "2026-04-09"
compatibility_flags = ["nodejs_compat"]
account_id = "2d3f34ca0a1426b30a7d07cf79bdac90"

# Force @stellar/stellar-sdk and @stellar/stellar-base to their CJS
# entry points, NOT the minified browser bundles.
#
# Without these aliases, esbuild's `browser: true` resolution (default
# for Workers) picks `dist/stellar-sdk.min.js` and `dist/
# stellar-base.min.js` — both of which inline their OWN copy of the
# Transaction/TransactionBuilder classes. Meanwhile any direct
# `require('@stellar/stellar-base')` from a sub-file (e.g.
# `stellar-sdk/lib/rpc/transaction.js`) ends up resolving to the
# CJS `lib/index.js` of stellar-base. Result: TWO copies of the
# `Transaction` class in the bundle, `instanceof` fails across
# them, and `assembleTransaction → TransactionBuilder.cloneFrom`
# throws `TypeError: expected a 'Transaction', got: [object Object]`.
#
# Aliasing both packages to their CJS entry forces every import to
# share one module instance.
[alias]
"@stellar/stellar-sdk" = "./node_modules/@stellar/stellar-sdk/lib/index.js"
"@stellar/stellar-base" = "./node_modules/@stellar/stellar-base/lib/index.js"

[[kv_namespaces]]
binding = "MPP_STORE"
id = "b0fa51efc09e4e708c6bd5061b0663e0"

# The AtomicStoreDO class is exported from src/index.ts (re-exported from
# src/mpp/atomic-store-do.ts). wrangler requires the class export to be
# reachable from the Worker `main` entry point — it is, via the re-export.
#
# On first deploy, wrangler creates the DO class automatically via the
# migration below. No manual provisioning needed.
#
# IMPORTANT: See "REMAINING FOR HUMAN" in the P1-3 task for the channel
# cumulative migration concern before cutting over.
[[durable_objects.bindings]]

# 2026-04-11 fixed XLM/USD rate, operator-updated. Sets the conversion
# ratio for XLM-denominated Stellar channels so the router stays solvent
# in its cross-asset broker role: agent pays N/XLM_USD_RATE XLM on the
# Stellar side, router pays N USDC on the Tempo side, broker net ≈ 0.
# Update this whenever XLM price moves materially (>5% in either
# direction) and `wrangler deploy` to roll forward. Only XLM channels
# are affected — USDC SAC channels (like agent2) ignore this value.
# See internaldocs/v2-todo.md#c and v2-session-session-done.md §5.1.
XLM_USD_RATE = "0.1533"

# ---------- x402 inbound over Stellar (Phase 1) ----------
# Non-secret defaults for the stellar.x402 branch of the proxy.
# The router runs an in-process x402 facilitator backed by
# @x402/stellar/exact/facilitator, receives agent USDC on Stellar
# mainnet at STELLAR_X402_PAY_TO, and pays the downstream merchant
# from the existing Tempo pool. See src/mpp/stellar-x402-server.ts
# and tasks/todo.md for design.
#
# Flip X402_ENABLED to "false" to disable the new branch entirely
# (classifyAuth falls through to passthrough for non-mppx credentials).
# No other changes required.
X402_ENABLED = "true"
# G... recipient address. Agent USDC lands here after settle.
# Public key only — must NOT be a secret. Operators can rotate
# this by flipping the value and restarting.
#
# Intentionally set to the SAME address as STELLAR_ROUTER_PUBLIC
# (the mppx pool). Both the MPP `WWW-Authenticate` dialect and the
# x402 `Payment-Required` dialect advertise this one address as
# payTo, so a single Stellar account receives USDC from both
# protocols. This keeps operator accounting simple — one pool,
# one reconciliation. If you ever split these (e.g. isolate x402
# funds into a separate account for independent risk control),
# update this comment AND the two call sites:
#   - src/mpp/stellar-server.ts:75  (getRouterStellarAddress)
#   - src/mpp/stellar-x402-server.ts:436  (buildX402PaymentRequiredHeader)
STELLAR_X402_PAY_TO = "GDK3AVW3YE6UL3J4WLNKBMP65KSY32YPUKIOC6PXW65XJ3LEG3YIDXXB"

# Secrets (set via: wrangler secret put <NAME>)
#
# STELLAR_ROUTER_PUBLIC          — G... Router pool address (receives agent USDC via mppx)
# STELLAR_GAS_SECRET             — S... Gas sponsor keypair (pays tx fees, low-value)
# STELLAR_GAS_PUBLIC             — G... Gas sponsor address
# STELLAR_X402_FACILITATOR_SECRET — S... Stellar x402 facilitator signer.
#                                  Builds + submits Soroban invoke txs for
#                                  the stellar.x402 branch's settle step.
#                                  Can reuse STELLAR_GAS_SECRET — same
#                                  account, different env var so operators
#                                  can rotate them independently if wanted.
# TEMPO_ROUTER_PRIVATE_KEY       — 0x... EVM key (pays merchants on Tempo)
# TEMPO_ROUTER_ADDRESS           — 0x... EVM address
# MPP_SECRET_KEY                 — random hex string used by mppx to HMAC-bind 402
#                                  challenges so returning credentials can be
#                                  verified statelessly. Rotate to invalidate all
#                                  outstanding challenges.
#
# Note: STELLAR_ROUTER_SECRET is NOT stored anywhere in the app.
# The Router pool private key is managed offline by the operator.

        // Voucher path: cumulative was already written atomically above.
        // Mark the challenge as used and return receipt directly (no long operation).
        await store.put(challengeStoreKey, { state: 'used', usedAt: new Date().toISOString() });

    51	export function createStellarPayment(env: Env) {
    52	  // Store.cloudflare with AtomicParameters produces Store.AtomicStore,
    53	  // which @stellar/mpp 0.7.0 requires for replay protection (update() CAS).
    54	  // The DO-backed doAtomicParams provides TRUE linearizable CAS — see
    55	  // src/mpp/kv-atomic-store.ts for the full race-safety proof.
    56	  const store = Store.cloudflare(doAtomicParams(env.ATOMIC_STORE))
    57	
    58	  const method = stellar({
    59	    currency: getStellarUsdcSac(env),
    60	    recipient: env.STELLAR_ROUTER_PUBLIC,
    61	    network: env.STELLAR_NETWORK as any,
    62	    rpcUrl: env.STELLAR_RPC_URL,
   140	  // and cumulative monotonicity. The DO-backed doAtomicParams provides TRUE
   141	  // linearizable CAS — see src/mpp/kv-atomic-store.ts for the race proof.
   142	  // mppx uses `stellar:channel:cumulative:*` and `stellar:channel:challenge:*`
   143	  // key prefixes which do NOT collide with our `stellarChannel:*` sidecar
   144	  // metadata or `tempoChannel:*` / `idempotency:*` KV keys.
   145	  // See internaldocs/v2-stellar-channel-notes.md §N4.
   146	  const store = Store.cloudflare(doAtomicParams(env.ATOMIC_STORE))
   147	
   148	  const method = stellar.channel({
   149	    channel: channelContract,
   150	    commitmentKey,
   120	  }
   121	}

   123	function kvGet(key: string): string | null {
   124	  try {
   125	    const out = execSync(
   126	      `npx wrangler kv key get --binding MPP_STORE '${key}' --remote`,
   127	      { encoding: 'utf8', cwd: REPO_ROOT, stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] },
   128	    )
   129	    return out.length > 0 ? out : null
   130	  } catch (err: any) {
   131	    const stderr = err.stderr?.toString?.() ?? ''
   132	    if (
   133	      stderr.includes('Value not found') ||
   134	      stderr.includes('404: Not Found') ||
   135	      stderr.includes('not found') ||
   136	      err.message?.includes('404')
   137	    ) {
   138	      return null
   139	    }
   140	    throw new Error(`wrangler kv get failed for '${key}': ${err.message}\n${stderr}`)
   141	  }
   142	}

   146	function shortKey(key: string): string {
   147	  // Show the full prefix (stellar:channel:cumulative:) then abbreviated address.
   148	  const prefixEnd = key.indexOf(':', 'stellar:channel:cumulative:'.length - 1)
   149	  const addrPart = key.slice(CUMULATIVE_PREFIX.length)
   150	  if (addrPart.length <= 12) return key
   151	  return `${CUMULATIVE_PREFIX}${addrPart.slice(0, 6)}...${addrPart.slice(-4)}`
   152	}

const LOG_PREFIX = '[stellar:charge]';

/**
 * Creates a Stellar charge method for use on the **server**.
 *
 * Verifies and settles SEP-41 token `transfer` invocations received as
 * pull-mode (signed XDR) or push-mode (on-chain tx hash) credentials.
 *
 * @see https://paymentauth.org/draft-stellar-charge-00
 */
export function charge(parameters) {
    if (!parameters.store) {
        throw new PaymentVerificationError(`${LOG_PREFIX} A store is required for charge mode. Provide a Store instance for replay protection and transaction hash deduplication.`, {});
    }
    if (typeof parameters.store.update !== 'function') {

codex
The patch imports modules that are not part of the diff, which blocks a clean build/deploy. It also cuts replay protection over to an empty Durable Object without preserving existing charge replay state.

Full review comments:

- [P1] Track the imported route modules — /Users/happyfish/workspace/rozov2/rozo-mpprouter/src/index.ts:30-34
  In the diff being reviewed, `src/routes/create-invoice.ts`, `src/routes/webhook.ts`, and `src/utils/cors.ts` are not added/tracked, but `src/index.ts` imports and calls them here. A clean checkout of this patch will fail to bundle with module-not-found errors before the Worker can deploy, so include those files in the patch or remove these imports/routes.

- [P1] Preserve existing charge replay keys during cutover — /Users/happyfish/workspace/rozov2/rozo-mpprouter/src/mpp/stellar-server.ts:56-56
  When this deploys over an existing production KV, this line moves the Stellar charge verifier to a fresh Durable Object, while the new seed helper only backfills `stellar:channel:cumulative:*`. Existing `stellar:charge:challenge:*` and `stellar:charge:hash:*` replay records in KV become invisible, so an already-used charge credential within its challenge TTL, or a previously used push/hash payment, can pass the empty DO replay check; migrate/dual-read those prefixes or quiesce traffic until old challenges expire before enabling the DO.
The patch imports modules that are not part of the diff, which blocks a clean build/deploy. It also cuts replay protection over to an empty Durable Object without preserving existing charge replay state.

Full review comments:

- [P1] Track the imported route modules — /Users/happyfish/workspace/rozov2/rozo-mpprouter/src/index.ts:30-34
  In the diff being reviewed, `src/routes/create-invoice.ts`, `src/routes/webhook.ts`, and `src/utils/cors.ts` are not added/tracked, but `src/index.ts` imports and calls them here. A clean checkout of this patch will fail to bundle with module-not-found errors before the Worker can deploy, so include those files in the patch or remove these imports/routes.

- [P1] Preserve existing charge replay keys during cutover — /Users/happyfish/workspace/rozov2/rozo-mpprouter/src/mpp/stellar-server.ts:56-56
  When this deploys over an existing production KV, this line moves the Stellar charge verifier to a fresh Durable Object, while the new seed helper only backfills `stellar:channel:cumulative:*`. Existing `stellar:charge:challenge:*` and `stellar:charge:hash:*` replay records in KV become invisible, so an already-used charge credential within its challenge TTL, or a previously used push/hash payment, can pass the empty DO replay check; migrate/dual-read those prefixes or quiesce traffic until old challenges expire before enabling the DO.
